## v0.340.325 broker request journal explicit commit flush

Every acknowledged broker request/recovery write now crosses an explicit post-commit durability boundary. PREPARE, submission markers, observations, lookup begin/results and reviewed interruption records prove the committed SQLite connection bytes against a stable bounded pathname fingerprint, reopen only the exact captured regular single-link main file, hash that writable descriptor before and after `fsync`, and recheck the exact path and bytes. A newly created entry also fsyncs its validated parent directory on POSIX; Windows retains the portable main-file flush because Python exposes no portable directory-fsync API. Flush failure, wrong-directory substitution and descriptor-byte mutation fail closed after commit. Retained request or pending-lookup uncertainty remains reviewable and keeps retry, submission, routing and all trading/release authority false. Exact PR #37 Windows regression exposed that the committed fingerprint omitted pathname ctime while the generic cold-file verifier retained it. Pacing and request journals now use a dedicated sidecar-aware comparison that normalizes only Windows pathname ctime while requiring exact identity, size, mtime, links and bytes. The mandatory bounded provider-budget evidence profile covers that native schema contract and this request journal. This is local explicit flush observation, not physical-power-loss proof, native Windows acceptance, provider/account/source acceptance, routing permission or release acceptance.

Exact PR #38 amended-head Windows evidence passed the corrected committed-fingerprint boundary, then failed with the fixed privacy-safe code `RELEASE_TRIAL_RECONCILIATION_ACCEPTANCE_CHILD_TIMEOUT` after that fixture exceeded the generic 240-second child ceiling. The reconciliation fixture remains unchanged; only that profile on native Windows now receives a finite 600-second ceiling, while every other profile stays at 240 seconds. This preserves bounded execution and failure sanitization and requires a fresh exact-source hosted run; it is not native Windows acceptance or a relaxation of any evidence check.

**Current development build: 0.340.325 (pre-release).**

The pre-timeout-correction amended Linux source passed 6,162 tests with 2 skipped and one existing dependency warning. The broker/reconciliation durability slice passed 666 tests with one existing dependency warning, and the Windows-schema/request/pacing/release-trial amendment scope passed 152 tests with one existing dependency warning. The current timeout/evidence/version/roadmap/Windows-workflow/package slice passed 253 tests with one existing dependency warning. All 14 browser-controller smoke scripts passed, PORTAL JavaScript parsed successfully, dependency integrity reported no broken requirements and deterministic roadmap regeneration matched the committed document. Sixteen bounded release profiles and saved evidence verify against 407 source files / `15a42e88e3135feaca45e47d779b0c08e5301822ec7c0836d2f2cd4482c634b6`; evidence SHA256 is `2df4e05daf8392621e02a80d14d1cde4288e074515f16b6ffc2c0b56e98250ee`. The 163-member wheel reports v0.340.325 and has SHA256 `591f11912837ee9056ac54a274e01fcf388ecf7f6f2a11f4fa8b59bc29488714`. Hosted security/Gitleaks and browser help passed on PR #38 exact head `623ba3038f407b5072e16b5c930e529435e0949f`; its Windows evidence reached reconciliation acceptance before timing out, while the complete regression remained active at the correction checkpoint. ## v0.340.322 provider budget content acknowledgement binding

Committed provider POST and GET budget writes now require the writable SQLite connection's serialized main bytes to equal two stable bounded sidecar-free pathname fingerprints before acknowledgement, and the same fingerprint must remain after connection close. Uncommitted returns are rejected before serialization. Injected connection-content mismatch and post-close metadata drift retain the committed pending reservation and keep automatic retry, capacity reuse and all trading/release authority false. This is local bounded content continuity, not explicit fsync durability, physical-power-loss proof, atomic directory exclusion, native Windows acceptance, provider/account/source acceptance, routing permission or release acceptance.

PR #34's complete Windows regression finished with 6,073 passes and one deterministic portable-separator assertion failure. The provider-result fixture now emits its already-contained relative manifest paths with POSIX separators on every host; fresh exact-source Windows execution remains mandatory.

Every legacy account-read pacing write now acquires the existing cross-platform one-byte owner lease before opening SQLite, while typed broker reads retain that lease across provider I/O. Content attestation runs only after the writer owns `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`. A 100-iteration two-process regression produced exactly one admission per head and only fixed fail-closed contender errors. This is cooperating local-process exclusion, not a distributed or provider-wide budget.

**Previous development build: 0.340.322 (pre-release).**

## v0.340.321 provider transaction acknowledgement binding

Provider POST and GET write contexts now retain the captured SQLite main-file identity through transaction finalization, exact-main pre-close validation and a post-close pathname sample. Existing and new stores reject uncommitted returns; during-transaction replacement or an injected post-commit identity fault fails closed without deleting the replacement or making the pending reservation retryable. Windows release trials now retain only reviewed fixed paper-tool failure codes, and the disposable provider-result fixture derives relative manifest paths against the caller's lexical root or that exact root's resolved spelling so long and 8.3 temporary paths cannot disagree or escape their scope. This is sampled local acknowledgement integrity and privacy-safe evidence correction, not atomic directory exclusion, native durability, provider-wide coordination, account/source acceptance, routing permission or release acceptance.

## v0.340.320 provider-GET store binding and first-write recovery

The shared local provider-GET budget now freezes its selected parent, binds its exact path/provider scope/digest through ownership and requires the captured regular single-link main-file identity plus exact SQLite main pathname throughout setup. An aborted first transaction removes only its exact captured empty sidecar-free file. Mutable aliases, replacement, foreign connections, provider-scope mutation and object retargeting fail closed without redirecting a write, deleting a decoy or poisoning an exact retry. ## v0.340.319 provider-POST store binding and Windows emergency child lease

The local provider-POST pacing journal now freezes its selected parent, binds the exact path, provider scope and digest through ownership, and requires the captured regular single-link main-file identity plus exact SQLite main pathname throughout setup and rollback cleanup. A mutable parent alias, inode replacement, foreign connection or retargeted store object cannot redirect a write or make cleanup follow the replacement path. Retargeting rolls back and fails closed. Windows run 43634033695 showed that the existing directory handle did not block parent rename, so the emergency latch now holds a validated fixed regular single-link child handle without delete sharing for the full lease and rejects directory, reparse or hardlinked guards. A fresh hosted Windows pass remains required. This is local path/store and emergency-lease integrity, not broker-wide pacing, account acceptance, routing permission, native durability or release acceptance.

## v0.340.317 exact Windows emergency-parent lease

The native emergency-latch lease now opens the exact public Windows parent path with `OPEN_REPARSE_POINT` instead of following a pre-resolved path, and validates the resulting handle as a directory without reparse attributes before any latch entry access. A late junction or symlink replacement therefore fails closed rather than leasing its redirected target. The Windows regression contract also now recognizes ordinary access denial when the still-open latch file itself blocks a parent rename; that is conservative prevention, not evidence that the sampled-identity fence failed. PR #28's Windows regression failure remains recorded and is not dismissed as flaky. POSIX directory descriptors and fsync remain unchanged. This is not administrator/ACL isolation, distributed coordination or physical-power-loss proof. Runtime OFF defaults, limits, account checks, fail-closed exits and emergency-stop independence are unchanged. No UI controls changed: seven beginner tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right hover help remain intact. Broker-paper, genuine-feed 72-hour trials, native ordinary-user Windows acceptance, funding/live/cloud and owner release approval remain outstanding. Implementation remains weighted 38% (30–45%, low confidence) and 0/9 stages independently accepted.

**Previous development build: 0.340.317 (pre-release).**

## v0.340.300 paper pacing main-connection binding

The paper-read pacing writer preflighted its public SQLite path before opening it, but it did not require the resulting connection to report that exact path as its only `main` database or compare the connection's existing serialized main content with the exact bounded public main file before application mutation. A pathname/connection substitution or faulty connection seam could therefore direct a valid-looking append at a different or changed store.

v0.340.300 pins the pre-resolved single-link main-file identity across connection and setup boundaries, requires one exact SQLite `main` binding, and compares bounded serialized connection bytes with a no-follow fingerprint of the public main file before any Nexus append. A retained rollback journal is recovered under the same lease; after `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` performs SQLite's recovery, the recovered connection and exact main-file bytes must agree before application mutation. Binding/content disagreements fail closed with fixed codes, and raw path errors at the size boundary are contained. Existing DELETE mode, size bounds, account/schema binding, hash-chain replay, ownership fencing, cooldown, pending-review and recovery requirements remain mandatory.

## v0.340.293 standalone emergency-root identity fence

The independent emergency command previously resolved the configured data root once but did not retain its filesystem identity across priority elevation and its later intent/SQLite phases. A concurrent public-root replacement could consequently receive a new halt and intent and allow the command to report `VERIFIED` even though a running process remained attached to the displaced root.

## v0.340.267 active research-refresh ownership fence

The opt-in research-refresh worker now actively revalidates its opened OS owner lease before bounded public collection requests, retained-data import, training and model-reference publication, and again after publication. If the public regular single-link owner path is replaced, the worker pauses as `OWNERSHIP_REVIEW_REQUIRED`, discards work before publication and closes its retired descriptor without acknowledging a clean stop. ## v0.315.0 verified and flushed paper recovery snapshots

Completed snapshot images are now file-flushed and then reopened through immutable read-only SQLite. `PRAGMA quick_check(1)` must produce exactly one `ok` row within the existing ten-second budget. The destination's regular single-link identity plus size and timestamp metadata must remain stable, and no rollback/WAL/shared-memory sidecar may appear. POSIX additionally fsyncs the parent directory; the receipt exposes `directory_entry_synced` so Windows never implies a capability Python cannot portably provide. Corruption, timeout, sidecar appearance, identity change or flush failure returns only stable `PAPER_SNAPSHOT_*` review diagnostics and removes the candidate without modifying retained snapshots.

## v0.314.0 bounded paper snapshot storage admission

Before creating a new snapshot file, the recovery boundary now scans at most 256 directory entries and verifies every retained snapshot candidate is a regular single-link file with no pending rollback/WAL/shared-memory sidecar. It refuses source files above 512 MiB, a 65th retained snapshot, aggregate retained bytes above 4 GiB, and free space below the current source size plus 64 MiB. Stable `PAPER_SNAPSHOT_*_REVIEW_REQUIRED` errors reach Portal as redacted 503 responses. Nothing is deleted, pruned, converted or retried automatically.

Real retained-capacity, aggregate-byte, directory-entry, source-size, mocked free-space, retained-link and sidecar tests require unchanged retained files and no candidate creation. ## v0.313.0 bounded and link-safe paper recovery snapshots

The paper-portfolio snapshot boundary now samples the source as a regular single-link file before SQLite opens it and requires the same identity after backup. Data-root, source and recovery-directory symlinks are rejected. Each candidate uses exclusive no-follow creation, requests owner-only POSIX permissions, is rechecked as a regular single-link file and is hashed through a no-follow descriptor. Any failed candidate is removed; existing snapshots are not overwritten, rotated or pruned. SQLite backup uses a ten-second busy deadline and maps only busy/locked exhaustion to redacted `PAPER_SNAPSHOT_DATABASE_BUSY`; Portal returns 503 rather than leaking driver text.

Real-lock, source/directory-link, hardlink and injected mid-backup tests require bounded refusal and cleanup. ## v0.253.0 reconnect cooldown completion-time anchoring

## v0.252.0 service-unavailable reconnect delay retention

The one-attempt Activity SSE transport now treats exact HTTP 503 like HTTP 429 only when exactly one bounded ASCII `Retry-After` delta-seconds or timezone-aware HTTP-date is present. Both paths close the response without reading its body and expose only a fixed private error plus the normalized bounded delay. Missing, duplicate, malformed, newline-bearing or greater-than-one-day instructions become nonretryable review; all other non-200 statuses remain review-only.

The private reconnect journal accepts the new fixed service-unavailable detail code under the same fences as rate limiting. Applied backoff remains `max(fixed_attempt_delay, provider_delay)`, exact lost-result acknowledgement replay binds the same requested delay, and an active or indeterminate emergency stop retains review-required closure with no due reconnect. Existing result rows remain readable. No method sleeps, loops, opens a second request or gains trading/funding authority.

## v0.228.0 shared-local provider POST budget

`PaperProviderPostBudget` keeps a bounded private hash chain under a fixed provider scope and OS ownership lease. Exact expected-head admission binds account, request head, payload and hashed client-order identity. The transport records one response-observed, zero-post not-sent or one-post unknown result; unknown and explicitly interrupted tails impose a full-day review block. A stale head, cooldown, pending/review tail, related journal path, changed ownership, malformed count or tampered chain fails closed.

`alpaca_paper_submission.submit_once` now requires this separate budget and expected head. It commits the provider reservation before the request uncertainty marker and both before POST. Failures after reservation are finalized without rerunning revoked permission/stop checks: a known pre-POST abort is retained as `NOT_SENT`, while every attempted POST failure is retained as `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`. The request remains original-ID lookup-only in either case. There is no automatic retry, capacity reclamation, broker quota claim or distributed ownership claim.

## v0.227.0 internal single-attempt paper submission boundary

## v0.205.0 all-retained exact activity-time capture upper bound

## v0.190.0 exact reviewed account-pacing recovery

`PaperReadPacing.recover_review_block` accepts only an existing blocked journal, its exact current head, the exact bound paper account, a live local ownership lease and two explicit operator confirmations. AUTH_DENIED, unsupported REVIEW_REQUIRED and INTERRUPTED_UNKNOWN tails remain immutable. An interrupted account read now remains review-blocked after its full-day cooldown until recovery is reviewed; the recovery event never shortens `next_allowed_at`, deletes history, changes the retained outcome, reclaims the 256-session cap, reserves admission, retries or continues automatically.

The installed/source `--paper-tool account-recovery` command exposes that bounded mutation without opening credentials, contacting a provider or starting the desktop. Missing/stale/foreign/unconfirmed inputs fail without creating a pacing journal or echoing private arguments. Recovery output keeps provider-wide quota, distributed ownership, routing, live orders, money movement and release authority false. Older binaries do not understand the appended recovery event; preserve a private backup, stop cooperating readers and use the matching or newer reader rather than rewriting or downgrading the journal.

## Historical versions
Earlier development iterations were labeled 1.x, 2.x, and 3.x while the application was still simulation-only. Those labels represented prototype development milestones, **not** production releases or compatibility promises. The development snapshot previously labeled **3.8.0** is now **0.38.0**. Existing Git commits, CI logs, and artifacts retain their original historical names and are not rewritten.

## Forward version policy
- **0.101.0, 0.102.0, 0.103.0, …:** milestone-level pre-release development. Versions may progress beyond 0.101.0 if necessary (e.g., 0.102.0); there is no deadline-based automatic 1.0 promotion.
- **0.x.y:** fixes, small coherent enhancements, and refactoring of that pre-release milestone. No live-money approval implied.
- **1.0.0:** first completed production release, only after all launch acceptance gates are evidenced and explicitly approved by the project owner.
- **1.1.0 onward:** features added after the 1.0.0 launch, maintaining documented compatibility where possible.

The primary implementation sequence is [LAUNCH_DRIVE.md](LAUNCH_DRIVE.md). Global opportunity expansion in [EXPANSION_INITIATIVE.md](EXPANSION_INITIATIVE.md) and secure computer-off operation in [CLOUD_OPERATION.md](CLOUD_OPERATION.md) are explicit staged goals, with data, venue, risk and hosting acceptance before activation. Low-latency autonomous *paper* execution and its reliability tests belong to the v1.0 critical path; real-money use is separately authorized.

## Required gates before 1.0.0
1. Stable packaging, installer, startup, configuration, upgrade and recovery tests.
2. Independently audited and reliable market-data freshness/provenance, gaps, replays and failure behavior.
3. Verified model-provider integrations, safe output validation, cost controls, and no uncontrolled order authority.
4. Chronological historical research using unbiased train/holdout splits, reproducible performance measurements and realistic costs.
5. Sustained autonomous paper-trading reliability with persistent accounting, reconciliation and automatic risk vetoes.
6. Secure, permission-scoped execution infrastructure independently tested with strict order lifecycle handling, idempotency and emergency stops.
7. Extended forward validation, operational monitoring, secrets management, independent security/risk review and documented recovery drills.
8. Secure provider-held funding and governed autonomous execution workflows for the agreed production scope: credential isolation, spendable-funds and cash/position reconciliation, transfer failure/duplicate handling and a separately authorized limited rollout. No release enables deposits or live orders automatically.
9. Secure computer-off operation for the agreed scope, including encrypted durable state, remote stop, distributed ownership and quota/outage recovery.
10. Explicit owner approval for production release and any *separate* authorization for live-money order activation.

A green CI build alone is not a release acceptance gate. Research profitability cannot be guaranteed. The current branch has **no real-money order authority**.

The guarded result binds all four observation manifests plus both component hashes. Its current-head variant samples once and never retries; a changed journal fails closed.

## v0.184.0 reviewed legacy retired-ID baseline migration

The application-closed order-offload tool now accepts a deliberately private `nexus.order-retired-id-baseline-review.v1` document for a legacy rotated source whose lineage predates the v3 retired-ID fence. The document must explicitly confirm a complete reviewed scope and bind its sorted, unique IDs to the exact source generation, epoch digest, replay digest and verified archive-manifest digest. It is rejected if any client ID is malformed, duplicated, out of order, above the fixed cap, collides at the private fingerprint layer or omits an ID still provable from the current archive.

`review-retired-id-baseline` performs a content-free read-only review. `prepare-replacement --reviewed-retired-id-baseline` then creates a v4 candidate whose replayed lineage binds the aggregate review digest and labels provenance `OPERATOR_REVIEWED_UNATTESTED`. Raw IDs remain only in the caller-selected private file; they are not copied to the candidate, manifest, CLI report or public evidence. Public repository auditing rejects the baseline schema even under an ordinary filename. Existing v1/v2/v3 candidates remain verifiable, and ordinary v3 preparation refuses an unnecessary baseline.

This closes the missing software migration mechanism, not the factual completeness gate. The source cannot attest IDs omitted by older rotations, so a real migration still needs an authorized operator and independent review of the private list. The operation remains halted, flat and non-installing, with broker, funds, live, cloud and release authority false. The read-only roadmap estimate remains about 40% (30–45%, low confidence), with 0/9 fully accepted stages; this bounded migration feature does not justify an estimate increase.

## v0.181.0 replay-bound replacement epoch lineage

Every newly prepared v2 replacement journal adds one durable `bind_replacement_epoch` command after it is initialized, portfolio-linked and halted. The content-free record contains a bounded generation number, the exact predecessor archive-manifest SHA-256 and the prior epoch SHA-256; its own digest is deterministically replayed through ordinary recovery and checkpoints. Candidate, install-recovery and installed-pair verification require these values to agree. A two-rotation regression proves generation 1 → 2 continuity while old v1 candidate, recovery and interrupted-install marker records remain readable.

Lineage does not copy retired order details into the live journal, delete any archive/recovery artifact, prevent arbitrary callers from deliberately reusing an old local simulation ID, resume the runner, connect a provider or grant funds/live/release authority. Full multi-epoch pressure, ID-policy review, native power-loss/private-volume/profile acceptance and exact-source CI remain open. No Portal control or modifiable parameter changed, so the seven tabs and black/bright-purple bottom-right help contract are unchanged.

The packaged-tools harness keeps the ordinary 30-second child limit and widens only the two expensive encrypted-evidence self-tests (observation crypto and sealed quote evidence) to a bounded 60 seconds; crypto parameters and runtime behavior are unchanged. ## v0.180.0 interruption-recoverable halted replacement installation

Exact v0.179.0 source `1e3cc62a031470b874bc9c4301e9f4dcd745b240` passed security 39415934750, all safety/browser/evidence jobs in 39415934794 and Windows regression/packaging/installer 39415934810.

`install-replacement` revalidates the exact halted/flat source, archive and candidate, creates a new external recovery directory containing the exact source database pair and a path/account-free manifest, then writes a persistent live-data rotation marker before replacing either live database. Ordinary gateway reads and writes refuse to open while the marker exists. A successful install verifies the fresh halted/flat pair before clearing the marker; an interruption requires explicit `recover-install`, while `rollback-install` restores a completed install from the same verified recovery copy.

The live journal regains ordinary command/ID capacity, but the source history remains in the verified archive and recovery copy. Nothing automatically deletes or retires those artifacts, resumes a runner, connects a broker, moves money or grants live/release authority. Portable Windows directory fsync is unavailable, so exact-source native power-loss/private-volume/profile/ACL acceptance and multi-epoch long-run pressure remain open. No UI control or parameter changed.

## v0.177.2 bounded shared-budget contention recovery

Exact v0.177.1 security run 39385781709 and safety/browser/evidence run 39385781778 passed. Windows run 39385781816 completed 3,379 tests but failed before packaging because concurrent shared-budget instances admitted 8 of 12 safe slots. SQLite writer lock timeouts had been conservatively grouped with expected cap exhaustion.

The budget now retries only typed SQLite BUSY/LOCKED contention for at most 30 monotonic seconds. Every failed attempt closes its connection and rolls back; a continued lock produces a fixed timeout code with no SQLite/path detail. The concurrency contract requires exactly 12 committed admissions and 18 exact exhaustion denials. The 36-request/hour limit, commit-before-transport ordering, explicit public-discovery start, UI, permissions and authority remain unchanged. Exact-source Windows execution remains required.

Local validation passed 3,401 tests, the 215-test focused set and all sixteen source-fingerprinted profiles. Saved evidence matched 347 files / `3a04e339dd8a1b92669d1a2dae2af3dc806b2cc9572edaac31738518006ce882`; report SHA256 is `2ed3f208329c276fe3a14d9faf3a5a81b965b1b94c194dd3b1e752c7e1de7d9b`. This does not replace exact-source Windows acceptance.

## v0.177.1 Windows offload handle correction

Exact v0.177.0 public-security run 39335584198 and its browser-help job passed, while the Windows release-evidence operations child failed. Its SQLite integrity connections were entered as transaction context managers, which do not close the underlying handles. Linux permitted rename/removal with the leaked handles, masking the native locking defect. v0.177.1 explicitly closes both read-only integrity connections before replay, rename and cleanup; a regression blocks publication until every tracked integrity handle is closed.

This patch changes no command semantics, user interface, editable parameter, permission, risk limit, account, provider, funding, live/cloud behavior or authority flag. Exact-source Windows regression/release evidence, standalone/installed command smoke, packaging/installer and security workflows remain mandatory.

Local validation passed 3,399 tests, the 152-test focused set and all sixteen source-fingerprinted profiles. Saved evidence matched 347 source files / `5d7aec1cd4980de8e8b330b319cf04ac8d95368bccf221db4a913b948367de21`; report SHA256 is `262de076ea23e39df01e7be2a1c85529e796f5f3fbd877861f27e35117b1f258`. This does not replace exact-source Windows acceptance.

## v0.176.0 sustained-trial capacity readiness

The existing read-only Paper Trial Readiness action now combines retained quote/session headroom with the replayed durable order journal's ordinary-command and intent-ID capacity. It reports whether the current reserve can admit the next runner cycle and separately computes a conservative upper bound that assumes every policy-cadence cycle consumes the runner's full admission reserve. That bound is a fail-closed capacity diagnostic, not predicted strategy activity, expected orders, performance or market realism.

Missing histories remain absent and no check starts the engine, creates a session, reserves storage, opens credentials, contacts a provider or changes an account. The view keeps all authority flags false. A 72-hour quote window fits the current 100,000-row evidence cap on a fresh profile, while its deliberately conservative order bound exceeds current command/ID limits and therefore makes the physical-offload/long-run gate visible instead of implying sustained readiness. Exact-source Windows, browser/help, release-evidence and security CI remain required.

Local validation passed 3,387 tests, the 89-test focused set and all sixteen source-fingerprinted profiles. ## v0.177.0 verified physical-copy foundation

The new `order-offload` installed/source tool requires an explicit application-closed confirmation and refuses anything except a stopped, flat, reconciled journal with at least one logical archive batch and no active command epoch. It rejects SQLite sidecars, symlinks, hard links, special files, missing linked portfolio state, oversized inputs, an existing or in-tree destination and malformed/tampered output. It stages a new private directory, copies both SQLite databases, records content hashes and bounded replay counts, runs integrity checks plus complete archived-order replay, and verifies that the source bytes/replay remained unchanged before and after publication.

The output is deliberately copy-only. Source deletion and `capacity_reclaimed` remain false; there is no compaction, rotation policy, automatic resume, broker access, credential access, order submission or permission change. The operations release profile now includes a disposable verified-copy fixture, and the packaged tool smoke runs create and verify through the executable command dispatcher. See `ORDER_OFFLOAD.md` for operator scope. No Portal button or editable parameter was added, so the seven tabs and existing black/bright-purple beginner-help contract are unchanged.

Exact-source v0.177.0 Windows packaging/installed-tool, safety/browser/evidence and public-security workflows remain mandatory, as do private-volume/native-profile recovery acceptance, multi-epoch rotation/compaction, sustained long-run trials, actual broker-paper reconciliation and every funding/live/cloud/independent/owner gate.

## Enforcement
Application version, package metadata and Windows installer version/output filename must match. Automated tests guard against accidentally labeling an unfinished preview 1.0.0 or 1.1.0. The hourly autonomous development task is instructed to keep all work within the 0.x prerelease series unless these gates are independently evidenced and the user explicitly directs the promotion.
